Anže Logar, Mr Andrej Chus, Mr Joseph Horvat from 16 till 21:00, Luka Mesec from 21:00 and Mr Branko Zorman until 12 noon. I say hello to everyone who's here! We're moving to 2. Agenda item - Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Public Guarantee, Survival and Disability to the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, summary procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the Minister for Labour, the family, social affairs and equal opportunities of Dr Anji Kopač Mrak.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u2	Thank you for your word, Madam President. Good morning, everyone! Dear Members and Members. before you is the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Public Guarantee, Maintenance and Disability Fund of the Republic of Slovenia, which provides for the accession of the Public Fund of the Republic of Slovenia for the development of staff and scholarships to the Public Guarantee, Maintenance and Disability Fund of the Republic of Slovenia. In May 2015, the Court of Auditors of the Republic of Slovenia issued an audit report on the effectiveness of the financing arrangements and the functioning of public agencies and public funds. At the Ministry I run, we have carefully examined the recommendations of the Court of Auditors, including calls to verify the eligibility of individual agencies and public funds and to analyse the cost aspects of their operations. The proposed legislative changes aim at the substantive and organisational transformation of both public funds, which are the only ones under the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities. Due to related work processes, work is expected to be optimised; good business practices of one fund can be transferred to a joint body. In the work area of the new Fund, the implementation of all policies that fall within the working circle of our ministry will be new. According to our assessment, the merger of the two public funds means a way to make it easier to function and to achieve the objectives set more quickly, to achieve dynamic and balanced growth in skills and development, to increase safety and business stability, to strengthen reputation and to increase the efficiency of business. The merger into one public fund also reduces the operating costs of both funds and consequently reduces the public financial expenditure. I would like to point out that the draft law contains only the necessary legal changes to bring together the operation of the two Funds under the auspices of the Ministry and that the activities relating to pooling of funds will not affect in any way the exercise of rights or the rights of the recipients of the funds of the two Funds. In the process of drafting and considering the law, we have listened as a proposal to your warnings and to the proposals of the Legislative and Legal Service, the state councillors and representatives of both funds. On this basis, the legislative text with the amendments has been further improved, and with the extended period of application of the law to the beginning of 2017 we have also provided ourselves with sufficient time for the funds to be able to prepare themselves thoroughly and implement all the necessary procedures for co-operation and single operation. I am convinced that there is an opportunity before us for the growth of two public entities under the auspices of our Ministry, so I would like to conclude by asking you to support the proposal for a law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u3	The draft law was considered by the Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Persons with Disabilities to be the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the President, Mr Uroš Pikl.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u4	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Colleagues and colleagues, Minister! At its 22nd meeting, the Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons considered the draft law amending and supplementing the Law on the Public Maintenance and Disability Fund of the Republic of Slovenia, submitted by the Government to the National Assembly. On the basis of a decision by the College of the President of the National Assembly, the law was examined following a reduced procedure. The Committee has also received the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Social Protection, Labour, Health and Disabled Persons, which supports the proposed amendments to the law. In the introduction, the applicant was told that, following a proposal for a law, he would join the Public Guarantee Fund, the Maintenance Fund and the Disability Fund as well. The proposal does not change the scope of rights, which is essential to beneficiaries and follows the principles of sustainability of public finances, the economic use of public funds, efficiency, proportionality and, last but not least, transparency. In 2015, the Court of Auditors of the Republic of Slovenia issued an audit report on the effectiveness of the financing arrangements and the functioning of public agencies and public funds, where, inter alia, it proposed to the Government to examine the adequacy of the existence of individual public agencies and public institutions and to choose, in setting up new or existing existing ones, a form that would be most cost-effective and substantively justified. In order to reduce the number of public sector entities, the proposed law ensures rational organisation and at the same time rationalisation of work, simplification of procedures and processes in public administration. A positive impact on public resources is expected, as is synergies in substantively similar processes, and there is no reduction in the rights of beneficiaries. The debate that followed highlighted the principle of agreement between the Fund and the work on the economic use of public funds and the sustainability of public finances. The question of the real savings of the country and why the integration of the larger fund into the smaller fund was also raised. It also asked the debaters how the information system will be harmonised, what costs and what will be the situation with the employees. In response: the accessions will not affect the procedures and existing content of the rights related to the operation of the above-mentioned public funds, nor will they affect the staff, as from the date of the accession, the new fund will take over all public servants, assets, assets, equipment, rights, obligations, unfinished business and everything else. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted first on the amendments as a result of the comments or opinions made by the Legislative and Legal Service and finally voted on and adopted all the articles of the bill. In the light of all that has been said and of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended amended legislation, as already said, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. Thank you very much.

The Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities considers this to be an example. We are inclined to combine the responsibilities and tasks of individual institutions in cases where unnecessary financial expenditure is eliminated for their operation, duplication of work, removal of unnecessary administrative obstacles and so on. When introducing the law, the Ministry refers to the audit report of the Court of Auditors of the Republic of Slovenia entitled The effectiveness of the financing arrangements and the functioning of public agencies and public funds of 18 May last year. It proposed to the Government, inter alia, to examine the adequacy of the existence of individual public agencies and public funds and to select a form that would be cost-effective and most substantively justified in establishing new or existing entities. The latter, we believe in Desus, will have positive business effects, as the established good practices of each area can be passed on to other areas and can be improved or unified in individual operating systems. According to our assessment, some of the integration dilemmas that have emerged in this proposal of the law in view of the past experience of the merger of the guarantee and maintenance fund with the disability fund have already proved to be a positive synergy effect, in particular in recovery, irrespective of the very different content that the two funds previously dealt with. This is how the government seeks to ensure rational organisation and the work of public administration and to simplify procedures and processes. A positive impact on public resources is foreseen, including through the exploitation of synergies in substantively similar work processes. According to the Association Government, it will not affect the procedures and existing content of the rights related to the operation of the said public funds, nor will it affect the staff, as the new Public Fellowship, Development, Disability and Maintenance Fund of the Republic of Slovenia will take over all public officials, assets, equipment, rights and obligations, unfinished matters, documentation and archival material of the Public Fund of the Republic of Slovenia for the development of staff and scholarships. We have also adopted an amendment on the home working body, which has postponed the deadline for the implementation of the accession to the beginning of 2017, thus providing more time to prepare. A working group will also be set up, following government assurances, to ensure that the accession process is smooth and organised as far as possible, without unnecessary delays and complications. The amendment of the law is therefore more or less technical in nature. However, any expressed substantive problems that have been highlighted during the discussion will also be addressed through changes to the sectoral laws, as provided by the proposer. We hear calls from various institutions and stakeholders to rationalize the operation of many public funds and agencies in the Republic of Slovenia. One year ago, the Court of Auditors of the Republic of Slovenia also drew attention to this fact when it issued an audit report on the effectiveness of the financing arrangements and the functioning of public agencies and public funds and the Government, inter alia, proposed to examine the adequacy of the existence of individual public agencies and public funds and to select a form that would be most cost-effective and substantively justified when establishing new or transforming existing entities. The proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Public Guarantee, Maintenance and Disability Fund of the Republic of Slovenia follows these calls and is therefore considered welcome in this regard and will be supported in our Group of Social Democrats. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u23	Mr Jan Škoberne, the Group of Social Democrats, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u24	Dear Mr President, colleagues and colleagues! An appropriate relationship between safety and flexibility, which must be ensured to all labour market players, is essential in the drafting and adaptation of labour legislation. With a global view to ensuring flexibility for employers, it is so crucial that an adequate level of safety for employees is also ensured, and a broken relationship on the one hand or on the other creates negative effects on the development of the economy and society as a whole. In Slovenia, the last labour reform took effect in 2013 and the changes were aimed primarily at upgrading the system of safe flexibility by ensuring better protection of employees' rights, reducing labour costs, simplifying employment and forgiveness procedures and more effective monitoring and judicial protection. Two years after the adoption of the reform, we can see that the effects were mostly positive. Today, companies are more often employed than they are before the reform, and the increased employment in this category is most pronounced, and this is our greatest joy, among young people. One of the most up-to-date challenges in the labour market, however, is currently a growing gap between atypical forms of work and work under the regular employment contract. We have therefore prepared a document this year entitled Decent Work for All. We would like to encourage the debate on the causes and consequences of the use of these atypical forms of work and in social dialogue to create more appropriate solutions to the existing situation. Above all, through the concept of decent work, we want to put in place a framework for an effective regulation of the problem of exorcism and others; we need to stress that no less is the problem of abuse of people, employees who want to live with dignity from their honest work. It is essential, however, to ensure that both political parties and the government and, in particular, the Ministry of Field Affairs jointly implement solutions coordinated in social dialogue and on the principle of maximum balance of safety and flexibility, both from the employer's point of view and from the employee's point of view. The one-time, partial measures, which are above all not yet coordinated with the partners in social dialogue, are therefore contrary to what we are advocating by the Social Democrats. That is why we are more inclined than to inflation inspection measures to implement the concept of decent work consistently for all. For a sustainable and long-term solution is more urgent than actions that come and go. Finally, the most important balance between security and flexibility is the conflict between capital and work in its core. We believe that this conflict can be overcome. The tool for this can only be a responsible and inclusive social dialogue. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u25	The text is given by the United Left Group, for which Luka Moon.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u26	Thank you again, Mr President. Good-bye! We have a double practice in the prevention of imaginary jobs in Slovenia; on the one hand, we have a tax inspection. If he has a pen job or a job on bad luck, he'll be inspected by a tax inspector. For if he finds out that someone is employed in black, he says no work. The prohibition shall apply until the employer hands over the contract of employment to the employer, submit it to all relevant social security and settle all outstanding obligations. For the tax inspector, only one form of black work is forced by espions. The consequences for the worker are practically the same: the worker is not without insurance, but mostly secured with a minimum basis; he does not have the right to a paid hospital, to a paid leave and to a regress; he does not have the right to cancellation periods and to retirement. This means that in the case of forced espions, in those who are employed through fictitious agencies, and in those who are working in black, we have practically the same situation: the worker is injured, the country is damaged. Therefore, I repeat that if such a worker is lucky, an inspection will be carried out by a tax inspector who will charge the employer with the payment of all the wrongs; therefore, he will apply for insurance, hire him and so on. However, if the worker is less fortunate, the company will have control of the work inspector, where the worker will land in a significantly worse position. The decision of the Labour Inspectorate, contrary to the decision of the Tax Inspectorate, allows the employer two options, and may cancel the employee without any dismissal or payment, but may conclude an employment contract with him. Since employers live on the exploitation of workers, of course, they do not usually dismiss the worker first. They force him to sign a new copyright contract if he doesn't land, they hire him through the agency. If he doesn't agree to it, they hire him for a short period of time and pay him off through the afternoon espionage. The worker is severely injured here, and the same is true of the state, which is left with no contributions paid. If the worker also refuses to do so, he is dismissed, which is a grave violation of the Labour Relations Act. Labour law is based on the assumption that the working relationship exists when there are elements of the working relationship. With our proposal, which is, unfortunately, the last time before us, the practice of labour inspection would be aligned with the practice of tax inspection. So if we find an employer who violates labour law, the labour inspector, like the tax inspector now does, should hand over the employment contract and pay all the contributions. What we propose is essentially the realization of the idea that the Ministry of Labour has written in a decent work document, I quote, "the inspectorate, when determining the elements of the working relationship, can also impose on the employer the obligation to submit contracts of employment". Our proposal is only more universal because it also includes agency workers from illegal agencies. The procedure is similar to that of espies, only if the user does not extradite the contract in this case, but must offer to conclude the contract under certain conditions and the worker decides whether to hire the user. If he refuses, he remains employed with the Agency, but of course he must no longer work with the user. The position of the United Left is that the Coalition and the Ministry of Labour made a serious mistake when they did not join the legislative process. The Ministry received the initiative of the press union at the same time as all parliamentary groups. Moreover, last year the Ministry has called on the Labour Inspector and the Ministry has been aware of its error since 2014 when the same team proposed it. When the coalition and the government did not support our proposal or even wanted to talk about it, the employers received a clear signal that illegal exploitation could continue. This will go to a great extent to the detriment of employees, as there is a large-scale investigation in the media houses, only 49 of them have been found in the media house, and the union estimates that there are still 600 such external workers in the entire industry. The fate of all of these is now at stake. The reason is that the coalition and the government refused to listen. It seems that the debt write-off story is repeated, then we proposed a proposal, Minister Anja Kopač did not support it, because she ensured that she would prepare her own, but it was a fiasco. [[…]]

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u34	Thank you very much. The Law on the Inspection of Work, which we are considering today, highlights the issue of covered working relationships and infringements in the field of ensuring the work of user workers when employers do not have the right permission or are not registered in the relevant register. This issue is also highlighted by the Labour Inspectorate, which states in the 2015 Annual Labour Report that the trend in civil law contracts is in practice increasing, but the assessment of whether there are elements of a working relationship in a particular case is not easy in practice. Employers are always looking for new and new ways to cover this up and circumvent the legal ban. The proposal on the issue under consideration proposes two legal solutions. First of all, it proposes that the decision issued by the Labour Inspector in the case of the performance of work under civil law contracts and the elements of the employment relationship shall act as a decision on the existence of a working relationship between the worker and the employer. Unless the employer proves otherwise, it is assumed that the working relationship exists from the day the worker started working with the employer and is concluded for an indefinite period of full-time work. The applicant also proposes that the decision issued by the labour inspector be taken by the user when it finds that the posted employer who does not have a permit for this activity has been adopted by the user as a constitutional decision establishing a working relationship between the user and the agency worker. In the SMC Group, we point out that labour inspectors do not determine the existence of a working relationship. The determination of the existence shall be within the jurisdiction of the working court. The inspector shall determine, for the purposes of criminal proceedings, only the elements of the working relationship if there is a suspicion that such elements exist in a contractual relationship. In cases where the user accepts a posted worker from an employer who does not have a permit for this activity, consideration should continue to be given to the possibility of a legitimate way of remedying irregularities in such a detected infringement. The primary remedial measure is therefore the employer’s entry into the relevant register or records for foreign employers. We also point out in the SMC Group that there is no way that we can treat an infringement of the legislation governing labour relations and the labour market individually. When we are talking about the illegal use of atypical forms of work, we must, in the first place, address solutions for more effective supervision, informing the interested and the general public, addressing the causes of the use of atypical forms of work, including the flexibility aspect as a aspect of labour costs. To this end, the Ministry of Parts of the document For Decent Work has also prepared a starting point for amending legislation, namely the Labour Relations Act, the Labour Market Regulation Act and the Labour Inspection Act, and has drawn up appropriate solutions to both problems raised by the applicant in the paper under consideration today. In the SMC Group, we stress that these solutions, which we have already highlighted on the Committee and as the Secretary of State has promised, are expected by autumn. As stated in the Group of the Parties to the modern centre of the bill, we will not support it.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u36	Hello! Respected colleagues and colleagues, novelty or amendment of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, prepared in Novi Slovenije, bring two key innovations that will significantly improve the business environment and provide more jobs. They bring double status to Slovenian citizens and, second, limit the reimbursement to the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance due to the consequences of accidents at work. If I touch the double status myself first. Today you have many citizens who meet the conditions for retirement and could already receive a full old-age pension, but for various reasons they want to continue working. They want the activity they have been carrying out so far to be carried out in the future, or there are any other reasons why they want to open espies and do certain other services in peacetime. The current Slovenian legislation is such that pensioners must decide whether to give up part of their pension in order to be able to continue their activities. We think that this is the most stupid thing to do to limit citizens in this way, and therefore we allow this change to enable citizens to receive a full, 100 per cent old pension and at the same time open espies, to pay full contributions for full-time work. That this solution is correct, we are also encouraged by the last opinion of the Government in the White Paper on pensions, which clearly states that this is one of the solutions that must be implemented in the future. The fact that the non-implementation of dual status is harmful for citizens is also said by very specific data. As a result, only in January 2016 and February 2016, a thousand 942 espies were deleted from the basic register. Domestically, when espouses were available, whether they would continue their activity and receive only 20% of their pension or would close their activity or transfer it to another relative and receive a 100 % pension, more or less everyone decided on this second option. In some ways, the country has lost a thousand,942 individuals who have paid full contributions or at least part of their contributions to the pension and health allowance. With this dual status, we want to introduce a principle where it is understood that everyone employed is a dual benefit for the country; because it pays into certain state-owned cash registers and does not just collect from these state-owned cash registers. The second point that we are amending in this law is the limitation of reimbursements that the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance may require from employers due to the consequences of accidents at work. We've had this attempt this year. Unfortunately, he was a failure. In this attempt, we have further elaborated this law, drank it even more clearly, and wrote very clearly that when an employer causes an accident, an accident on purpose – I stress, on purpose – it must fully absorb the costs of all the pensions and treatments itself. However, if these accidents are due to unfortunate circumstances, we place a 20-fold limit on the minimum pension base for both legal and natural persons, which means nominally around €15 000. So, with the support of this law, we will take a major step forward towards improving the business environment and more jobs, so I invite you to support this change.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u43	The word is given by the Group of Members of the United Left, for her Luka Moon.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u44	Thank you for your word, Mr President, and a nice greeting once again. Even in the United Left, we will clearly vote against it. If this law had been adopted, there would have been no other consequence than that, for the first time, there would have been a drastic reduction in safety at work and, secondly, a new method of social dumping. In fact, the claim made by the Pension and Disability Insurance Office to the employer responsible for the injury of the worker is one of the key forces for employers to ensure safety at work. If this force were to be removed and further restricted, the most risky employers would clearly reduce the safety concerns of their employees. As the number of work accidents, including deaths, increased last year, according to the latest figures of the Labour Inspectorate, such a measure is totally unheard of. If this law were adopted, pensions would start to be paid to those who clearly do not need them; that is, those who are able and willing to work full-time and at the expense of all other insurers. This would not only increase the costs of the Pension Insurance Office, which already has insufficient income to pay pensions. Reduce and at the same time legitimize social dumping and unfair competition. How can a self-employed person who only survives on espionage income compete with someone who will receive a pension each month, a self-employed person with a pension, such as lawyers, could compete with other lawyers at dumped prices. The price dictated by self-employed journalists, translators or innkeepers, who would still receive their rent, may be so low that their self-employed colleagues will no longer be able to live with dignity from their activities. This is particularly worrying at a time when the espection of Slovenia is increasing dramatically. The data show that at the end of 2007 we had about 54,000 self-employed people after Surs, without farmers, and that in February this year there were 68 000, which is 25% more than 9 years earlier. That this is huge, we can see that we know that the number of employees at this time on the other hand has decreased by 8%. Thus, in summary, the number of self-employed persons increased by 25 % from 2007 to 2016 and the number of employees decreased by 8 %. As espouse increases, competition between more and more liquefied espouses is also increasing and espousing must be prevented but not further encouraged. Let me move on to the consequences of the law. The first is, as I said, a reduction in compensation liability. Since the proposal was last proposed to reduce employers' liability for injuries at work, data from the labour inspection for 2015 have been published. The Chief Inspector summarized them as follows: "We assess the safety and health situation in the Republic of Slovenia in 2015," I stress, "as inappropriate and inadequate. We do not see any encouraging progress in ensuring the safety and health of workers in the workplaces of the Inspectorate. The number of reported accidents at work in 2015 is increasing with respect to the previous year." These are the clear findings of the work inspection. Particularly worrying is the fact that more than 24 workers died from accidents at work last year, the largest since the beginning of the crisis. In short, the number of accidents is increasing, the Labour Inspectorate says that the safety situation at work is inappropriate and that there is no improvement in the situation at any point. The Christian Democrats have concluded from this that employers' liability for accidents at work must be limited and further abolished. In the United Left, for the same reasons, we have reached exactly the opposite conclusion; therefore, due to the increase in accidents at work, employers must be forced by new measures to comply consistently with health and safety measures at work. Therefore, when adopting the 2015 budget balance and when adopting budgets for 2016 and 2017, we have proposed an increase in resources for labour inspection. Since 2008, the number of work inspectors has decreased from 87 to 77, almost half of which are social inspectors. The result of the decisions of the second Janša government and two so-called left governments is that in Slovenia we have only good 40 working inspectors, while the number of business entities is increasing each year. Thus, 40 inspectors control more than 190,000 business entities. These figures alone indicate that their work is virtually impossible and that it is not possible to control, so it is not surprising that the number of exploitations is increasing and exacerbating each year, while the number of accidents at work is increasing and exacerbating. In doing so, the 2015 Inspectorate's report can also explain the structure of employers in whom the most fatal accidents occur. These data show that safety is most at risk in small businesses, i.e. those that are growing the most. And these companies are in the category of companies that the proposal of Christian Democrats would best protect by limiting compensation liability. As I repeat, colleagues from New Slovenia, there were 24 deaths last year, of which only 4 were in companies with more than 250 employees and 12 were in companies with less than 10 employees, i.e. micro-enterprises. In short, safety at work is the first time a problem can't be denied. In addition, I stress that the largest is right in the smallest companies. The National Assembly should not play with the health and life of employees, so we expect the Chamber of Commerce and Industry and its parliamentary representatives to stop such provocations and we expect the coalition to reject this proposal in a consistent manner. Now, the second problem I've pointed out before is dual status, i.e. retirement. In the United Left, we reject all proposals, not only those that we hope will not be accepted, but also government proposals that would turn the solidarity pension system into a pension. The pension is for those working people who can no longer survive their work. The law, however, assumes that this moment will occur at the age of 60 if the worker has worked for more than 40 years and in other cases later. If the worker has the opportunity and ability to work, he does not need to retire unless he is a public servant and loses his job after the ZUJF. If a worker retires in part, he is entitled to a partial pension. If he retires in full, he can perform occasional work, he can also be the procurator of his company and so on. However, if he wants to work as a self-employed person for a full-time job, he is grateful, but he cannot be in a state where he can still pay his pension. Because there is no pension in our system for saving in the past, we have a solidarity pension system. Employees and self-employed are not savers who voluntarily pay premiums. With us and other modern countries, we have a flow system in which contributions are spent on current pensions. Since current contributions are not sufficient to pay pensions, we need to ensure a difference from the budget. It's called intergenerational solidarity. Every form of income is in conflict with this system and we undermine it. As I said at the outset, pensions are particularly problematic because, if we put in place an arrangement whereby full-time self-employed people can also receive pensions, this would mean that they are placed in an unequal position with other self-employed people. If we have such, say, a self-employed journalist who works full-time and receives a pension while on the other hand a self-employed journalist who does not receive a pension is this second, clearly, competitive advantage and can work at a lower price than his non-retirement colleague. That's the problem. The problem is that we will reduce the prices that self-employed journalists can pass on without rent and we will introduce some form of social dumping, thus putting them in an impossible position, and now their situation is not very envying. Therefore, as we say, we cannot support this law in the United Left. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u45	The New Slovenia Group of Members - Christian Democrats, Mrs Iva Dimic, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u46	Dear President, Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! In our core programme, Christian Democrats advocate the inclusion of European standards in our labour law regarding the provision of working conditions, working hours, leave, protection at work, conditions of employment or termination of employment. However, working conditions that go beyond these standards must be addressed in social dialogue between employers and workers or their associations or unions. The State must invest more resources in promoting health in the workplace, raising awareness and informing employers and workers and should result in fewer accidents and consequently less costs for the treatment or payment of pension and disability benefits. In New Slovenia, of course, we support the amendment of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, which we added to the parliamentary treatment, since the promised systemic solution of the coalition was not yet available at this time. The key solutions presented by the novelty are: the regulation of the scope of the regressive claims of the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance, PPIZ, so that the employer is liable only if he violates the rules on safety and health at work on purpose or because of gross negligence when the cause of injury on the part of the worker or in cases of minor negligence is not. The legal limitation of the amount of the compensation, on a maximum of 20 times the lowest pension base, except in the case of an indemnity, and the arrangements for the dual status of pensioners. The application of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office to employers is regulated by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act, ZPIZ-2. The regulation is similar to the regulation of the regression claims of the Institute for Health Insurance of Slovenia to employers. We have already discussed them, and the proposal of New Slovenia was unfortunately not adopted. The problem in the area of excessive and unpredictable regression claims has not been the case since yesterday. Traders and small entrepreneurs have been alerting them for years. The fact is that current legislation and practice are such that a specific employer can have well-ordered internal acts, rules and education for workers in the field of safety and health at work, including workers can be well informed about all these rules. However, failure to comply with the instructions, non-use of safety equipment or any other work accident and, in the worst case, a small employer, who is generally economically weaker, may face such a high regressive requirement by the Pension and Disability Insurance Office that his existence is compromised. Let us note in particular that in the event of the closure of trades or businesses, all, both employers and workers, may be at risk in the case of independent entrepreneurs who respond to all their assets. The second essential proposal to support New Slovenia's adoption of the law is the fact that, by limiting the regressive claims to a maximum of 20 times the minimum pension base, the basis for the creation of insurance products by commercial insurance companies is to be based. Currently, craftsmen and entrepreneurs cannot insure the risk of regressive claims in commercial insurance companies. Even with regard to the regulation of the dual status of pensioners. Christian Democrats believe that the current solution is criminal and has created inequalities before the law. In our view, each insured person must be able to continue his or her activity as an independent entrepreneur or set up, while receiving the full retirement pension to which he or she is entitled. Why would the state prevent someone who would want to do this after retirement? We're talking full retirement. We estimate that this can only be good for the pension fund, as the full contributions of these insurers will be an additional inflow, which the state has given up with the government novelty. We also estimate that such a way of resolving dual pension status is more appropriate to pensioners than the one recently introduced by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, as it encourages insured persons who would like to remain active even after meeting the conditions for old and early retirement to do so legally. Members of New Slovenia will support, in their votes, the amendment of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u47	The text is addressed to Mr Saša Tabaković, the Group of the Modern Centre Party.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u48	Honorable President, Ministry, colleagues, colleagues! The version of the novel Pension and Disability Insurance Act, discussed today, refers to two solutions that we have discussed in the National Assembly in the last few months. The first solution refers to the dual status of insurers or independent entrepreneurs, which proposes that each insured person be able to continue his or her activity and receive the full retirement pension to which he or she is entitled. The second solution, however, refers to the regression claims of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office relating to the employer's liability when there is damage at work or occupational illness due to non-compliance with the safety and health rules at work. The Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance may require compensation for damage caused by disability, physical impairment, the need for foreign assistance and service or the death of an insured person on purpose or because of negligence. The novelty proposes that in this case the employer's responsibility should be excluded if it is a matter of ease and that the amount of compensation should be limited up to 20 times the lowest pension base. Restricting compensation upstream following a new proposal from the law would no longer be possible when the employer acted on purpose and it is also proposed to limit the employer's liability to the worker when the damage occurred on purpose and in this case the perpetrator is obliged to settle it. The proposed solutions relating to the dual status of independent entrepreneurs are considered to be comparable to those in other Member States of the European Union, although we can find that the European Union members have different arrangements. In some cases, dual status is limited in some way by profit from the pursuit of an activity or additional earnings while receiving a pension at the same time as in Germany, or by any new employment contract, such as in Poland, and there is no difference in the treatment of independent entrepreneurs and workers in a working relationship, such as in Austria. It should also be pointed out that a dual status mother is being tried before the Constitutional Court and therefore the amendment of pension legislation in a dual-status work is not appropriate at this time. In the case of regressive claims solutions, we must reiterate that the current pension and disability insurance arrangements include both liability for the damage caused by gross negligence and general negligence. The format of the fault is determined in accordance with Article 135 of the Bond Code. However, the cause of the injury is required to demonstrate that it acted with special care in its handling of the injury. It should also be pointed out that the employer does not respond when the full liability is on the employee’s side after the applicable scheme, since the pre-condition for the employer’s liability is also a causal link between the illegal conduct or omission and the occurrence of injury. Finally, I should like to point out once again that expenditure from the pension and disability allowance which is not covered by the payments is covered by the State budget, by a reduction in liability and by the proposed limitation of the amount of compensation up so that it is a burden for other insurers. Because of the above-mentioned proposal for legislation in the Group of the Modern Centre Party, we cannot support it. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u49	The text is given by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, Mr Marijan Pojbič.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u50	Before us is the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Pension and Disability Insurance, which proposes two solutions. First time. A proposal to regulate the dual status of insured persons for pension and disability insurance in such a way that each insured person has the opportunity to continue his or her activity and receive a full old-age pension when he or she fulfils the conditions or establishes a new activity while receiving a pension, without already reducing his or her pension. And a second time. It is proposed that the employer's liability under the Pension and Disability Insurance Office would be acceptable if the employer violates the rules on purpose or because of gross negligence, but not in cases where the cause of injury on the part of the worker or in cases of minor negligence. The law will be supported by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group in its first reading for the following reasons. We agree that the pension is a right acquired under the conditions met on the basis of the contributions paid. It follows that everyone, irrespective of the activity he carries out, has the right to a pension when he fulfils the conditions for doing so. Where the pensioner continues to operate, this means only an additional payment to the pension and disability allowance and an added value for the social product. Many pension recipients do not choose to carry out their activities or carry out their activities in black because their pension would fall disproportionately depending on how much work they would have to do in order to compensate for their pension. The current regime also provides for the possibility of abuse of the right to a partial pension, since the payment of a partial pension depends on the amount of hours paid and not on the actual payment made to carry out the activity. In our view, the proposed solution deserves further legislative treatment, in particular to verify its financial impact, which, according to experience from other European countries, should even be positive. However, the second solution proposed by this law has already been addressed in the National Assembly and has not been sufficiently supported. The aim is to relieve the employer of the regression claim by the Pension and Disability Insurance Office in the event of damage to the worker due to the minor negligence of the employer. We are fully aware of the fact that the expansion of the liability of compensation affects mainly smaller craftsmen and smaller private employers, who may also be affected by such a regressive request from the institution. The solution is partly acceptable to us, but only if it is clearly determined, on the other hand, that the disabled worker will in any event be compensated for the damage caused by the minor negligence of the employer in the injury to the work process. The next legislative process could also resolve this dilemma. I therefore call on the other parliamentary groups to undermine the further legislative process and we are working together to find solutions that will be acceptable to all: workers and employers and those who wish to continue their activities even after meeting the conditions for an old pension.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u108	Thank you for your word, chairman. Dear Members, honourable Members, distinguished Secretary of State, welcome! In April 2014, the National Assembly adopted an amendment to the Law on Prevention of Work and Black Employment, which introduced, inter alia, a system of valuations for the implementation of personal supplemental work. In practice, this means that a person performing personal complementary work has to notify and obtain a value. The price of the valuation is €9, of which €7 is earmarked for pension and disability insurance, €2 for occupational injury insurance and sickness insurance. In individual cases, the contractor must pay the value. This is when the contractor does a certain job for him, such as child protection, household assistance, training and the like. It must be paid by each contracting authority individually and for each month separately, which may mean more valuations per month for the contractor, depending on the number of subscribers. In cases where the contractor manufactures and sells domestic and art products, manual works, collects forest fruits or herbs, grinds cereals and so on, the contractor must pay the valuation. As a rule, this means one value per month. This system is supposed to work legally, and this would enable the operator to enjoy basic social security. One valuation is expected to bring one day of retirement to the contractor. This applies to those who are in the period of their employment; this does not mean an additional retirement period for pensioners. The system for them is therefore not encouraging at all. What were the predictions of Minister Anje Kopač Dark when the system of valuations was introduced? She predicted that the work would be reduced to black. Is it true? Let's look at the data. The Minister announced that the number of registrations for personal supplemental work would double, which would amount to about 18,000 registered operators of personal supplemental work. According to the Ipes website, on day 18. 5. 2016 on the list of individuals carrying out personal supplementary work, only 6 000 84 individuals, i.e. only a third of what the Minister announced. It is more obvious that the introduction of valuations did not encourage individuals to register their personal complementary work; rather, it did not encourage individuals to register their own complementary work. The Ministry has promised that an additional EUR 2 million would flow into pension and health care if 18,000 workers were registered. What's the reality? In 2015, only EUR 158 000 of the cash register, of which about 123 000 and about 35 000 for health insurance, went into circulation. According to the Ministry of Labour, the cost of introducing the valuation system was EUR 200 000 by the end of 2015. Of course, current costs, administrative costs, labour costs per administrative unit, the APIS, the Financial Administration and so on must also be included. And what we can see from this – that the introduction and operation of the system of valuations consumes significantly more than it returns to the pension and health allowance through contributions. The system of valuations is an exceptional opportunity to cover up work in black, as it establishes a framework that can actually be misused to increase the grey economy and work in black. The actual work and actual payment are not important under the existing system. All we can say is that the attempt to introduce valuations was a very poor attempt to fill the budget hole. Therefore, in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group, we propose to abolish them. The main solutions we are proposing are as follows. First, to delete the articles relating to the legal basis of the system of valuations and to replace them by the arrangements in force before that; only a restriction on the income of the supplementary work shall be maintained. Secondly, we propose that persons performing personal supplementary work be afforded a voluntary choice in terms of pension and health insurance; this means that a person carrying out personal supplementary work could be insured for pension and disability insurance of the amount to which he or she will choose, but at least at the rate of seven euros per month. There would also be voluntary insurance for occupational injuries and diseases in the amount of the health insurance premium. Thank you.

We believe that the system already in place has a positive impact on the economy and individuals. The proposal by the proposer is based on a flat-rate assessment of the shortcomings of the system so far; it is certainly premature for an assessment within one year of implementation; the proposed solutions of the proposer do not follow the objectives and principles of the bill itself; in particular, the solutions proposed by the proposer as an alternative are non-systemic, in many ways incomprehensible and also unfulfilled in practice. ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u114	Thank you, Vice President. Thank you very much. The proposal, as we have heard, refers to the performance of personal complementary work. Most of this work is done as household aid, but the real social problem in personal complementary work is not recording, but the fact that some are forced to do it; and under any circumstances. We do not have official information on this, but we can conclude on the basis of life experience. Contractors of such services are generally households with above-average incomes, domestic work is carried out mostly by women, in particular unemployed workers, low-wage workers and low-income pensioners. They do this job for bad pay, mostly in black. At the same time, they are increasingly competitive with various cleaning services, which often act as illegal labour agencies. Therefore, the first condition is that personal complementary work will also be dignified, i.e. properly paid and respected, regulation of the social situation of women on the side of society. Poverty is a complex problem, but there are simple solutions for it. All these solutions are under the authority of the Government and also the National Assembly. Everything is feasible here and now if the coalition decides to represent the interests of socially disadvantaged people. There are three measures in the grave. The situation of the long-term unemployed and low-income pensioners will not improve any economic growth, but rather financial social benefits. It is the coalition's responsibility to raise these benefits to at least the minimum cost of living. As is known, these costs were last calculated in 2009 and amounted to EUR 385 for a single adult. It is less known that Dr Anja Kopač Dark, Minister of Labour, signed a social agreement on behalf of the Government last year. It also committed itself to recalculating the minimum cost of living this year and to align the amount of social assistance with that calculation. As far as we know, the Ministry of Computing is not planning to perform. Nevertheless, it is clear that the minimum income should be raised to at least EUR 385 per month. It is also known that up to €90 million a year is needed to do so, which is about as much as would bring about a rise in the rate of corporate tax on profits prepared by the Ministry of Finance. This is, of course, realistic, provided that the government and the coalition will devote these 90 million to the socially disadvantaged. Currently, the Finance Minister proposes spending this 90 million on tax relief for about 10% of employees with the highest wages. However, the situation of the socially disadvantaged could be significantly improved immediately and without further State indebtedness. In black work, even in personal complementary work, people are often forced to work, although they are socially at risk but do not receive social assistance. They are often pensioners, in particular pensioners, who renounce social assistance or the protection allowance due to the Pahor-Svetlik reforms of 2010 and 2011. As we know, poverty is the most common among women over 65 years of age, especially in the countryside. In 2010, Pahor’s government abolished state pensions of more than 15,000 people. The second blow followed in 2012 with the new Social Security Benefits Act. Since then, social assistance and a protective allowance must be paid back consistently. Real estate owners have therefore given up massively on social benefits, in particular on a protective allowance. In such circumstances, many are forced to do every job under any circumstances. The Pahor's government has therefore legalised social dumping. If we want personal complementary work to become dignified, as they like to say at the competent ministry, the reform of Pahor must simply be abolished. Let me remind you that in the coalition agreement, the government has stated that the beneficiaries of a lower-value property security allowance will be burdened by the ban on the disposal of property. Within almost two years of her arrival in power, she has not done so yet. The third measure that would be necessary to improve the situation of socially disadvantaged people in general and the regulation of personal complementary work in particular is respect for labour law. Let us say that we will improve the situation of persons, especially women, who perform complementary work. In this way, offers of additional work will be less and workers will not be forced to work under any conditions; however, we will not solve the problem until we prevent unfair competition from various cleaning and similar services. And many, even in the public sector, we can see what is called the segmentation of the labour market. During the day they clean workers who are sometimes dressed in uniforms and who are employed in a particular organization, another team of young girls who are not in uniform comes in this afternoon and work with the same means, probably via some sort of cleaning service. Some things may be better arranged, but in general, cleaning services are de facto work intervention agencies, although they do not have permission to do so. It is similar with different types of complementary work, such as seasonal work. Until we effectively prevent illegal exploitation, the payment for such work will be shamefully low. One of the key solutions is the regulation of the competence of the work inspection, which we have proposed in the United Left; of course, the coalition has slightly refused. For the end, we need to define ourselves until concrete solutions. In detail, there is no point in starting because, if I quote the opinion of the Government, "solutions are neither systematic nor comprehensible nor enforceable". If we were already going to replace the values, it would have to be done in another way. So the key question is whether an end would make sense at all. The analysis I'm taking from the government's opinion shows no. The number of persons active in the personal supplementary work increased by at least twice after the new system was put in place. So the valuations covered a certain segment of people who had previously worked in black. During the first year, the health and pension bills received around €160,000 at the expense of the valuations. The cost of introducing the new system was around €200,000. It should be borne in mind, however, that these were one-off costs to be paid only on the medium term. Any major change in the system should change the information system and adapt the procedures for which additional financial resources should be provided. The evaluation system may not be the best possible system, but it obviously works now, so without very good reasons and concrete alternatives it is not meaningful to change it. When assessing the effectiveness of the evaluation system, we must also take into account that the main purpose of the valuations is not to collect social contributions, but to legalise complementary work. This system does not generate large revenues because traffic is also relatively small. The benefits of legalisation must be borne in particular by those who carry out this activity. On the one hand, because unfair competition is to be reduced; on the other hand, because these incomes are at least recognised by the pension. In view of the last decision of the Constitutional Court, it is clear that voluntary inclusion in compulsory pension insurance does not compensate for automatic inclusion on the basis of the valuations. Since the law is therefore, in the opinion of those who are supposed to enforce it, incomprehensible and non-executive and because there are no valid reasons why the system is being shut down, we will vote against it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u117	Thank you very much. Ms Janja Sluga will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u118	Thank you very much. The new Law on Amendments to the Law on Prevention of Work and Black Employment, which we are considering today, is primarily referring to the debate on the grey economy and the principle Every work counts. In the case of undeclared work and also in the case of undeclared transport we come to a common point, this is personal complementary work and valuation. As from 1 January 2015, the value has become a condition for the legal performance of the work and its value is fixed at a flat-rate amount of nine euro. Complementing the various forms of work and introducing the contribution rates also entered into the social security and compulsory insurance system by operators of personal supplementary work. With the application of the principle, it is also important to point out, in addition to providing pension and disability insurance, health insurance and occupational disability insurance, that in practice the value of the pensioner also brings pensionable age. The bill, however, removes the current system of valuations and proposes the establishment of a specific method of voluntary inclusion in compulsory pension and disability insurance. Persons performing personal supplementary work could, in accordance with the amendment under consideration, be secured for the same extent as will be provided under the current valuation-based regime. However, it proposes different bases and different recognition of rights, as provided for in the general pension law and the law governing prevention and employment in black. The applicant also provides for a means of inclusion in individual insurance, such as pension and disability insurance, special cases insurance and occupational health insurance and occupational injury. In doing so, we are missing the definition of the scope of rights in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre. Article 3 of the current Law on Pensions and Disability Insurance provides, for example, that compulsory pension rights, disability rights, the right to an annual allowance and the right to an allowance for assistance and service are guaranteed by compulsory insurance. However, the proposed option of selecting inclusion in special cases insurance may prove less favourable to the operator of the personal supplemental work, since insurance for special cases of insurance is not considered to be during the insurance or pension period. A proposal for a law would carry out the notification of a personal supplemental work on administrative units, which would also lead the list of performers of personal supplemental work; and not more on the iPad than so far. In doing so, we must point out that the latter solution would generate additional costs at least by establishing a new information system in administrative units. In the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre, we believe that one year after the introduction of the values, the positive effects of the existing system can be discussed. Since the introduction of the system of valuations by May this year, around 3 000 new notified providers of personal supplemental work and a good 24 000 issued valuations have been issued. The total amount of payments in 2015 was around EUR 158 000 on the basis of the valuations. The results so far are considered good, but only in the next few years will we be able to give a realistic or long-term assessment of the success of the evaluation system. As stated in the Group of the Parties to the modern centre of the bill, we will not support it.

If we stop this, the story that we had before the valuations, when different forms, just because of the cost aspect that the proposalr is talking about, has been competing with each other. And that was very bad. And with this system of valuations, it just wanted to go away. But if we now eliminate the system of valuations, we have a system like we had before, which unfortunately was not fair. As for the distorted system that we had before, it was that employers used this system for their own benefit, for their own expense; on the other hand, workers or individuals who decided to do some particular work had to do so, even though they knew that this was the worst for their retirement period, even the extra-deserved pension period. And that's why it's right that we have a system of valuations. I'm talking about thinking. We can talk about different things, whether the costs are justified – for now they show that they are – which I think is justified. But to abolish the system of valuations means simply attempting to abolish some mental code of our community, that we have decided that every work counts; and that in this sense we are more just. That's all. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u164	Dear Mr President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! Music quotas have been prescribed for many years and remain as they are with this bill. The purpose of the law now in force was to eliminate the anomaly afforded by the legal dictatorship, which was written in such a way that it could be abused. Most of the prescribed quota for Slovenian music was broadcast by radio stations at night. The current Media Act, which was adopted in the National Assembly on 28 January this year and entered into force on 9 April, provides in Article 86a, first paragraph, that at least 60 % of the share referred to in Article 86, first and second paragraphs shall be transmitted within a broadcasting period between 6 and 19 hours. It is said that, according to the current law, of all songs that will be rejected within 24 hours, the mandatory quota of Slovenian songs should be calculated and then 60% of this should be rotated between 6 a.m. and 19 p.m. Following the adoption of the current law, it subsequently appeared that national RTV radio stations would have difficulties in meeting this statutory share. Why? Because they have significantly more speech than a music programme in individual cases during the daytime, and it would be difficult to implement the mandatory quota for Slovenian music. With the bill we are considering today, we amend Article 86a by introducing the obligation to achieve the prescribed share in view of the specific volume of broadcasting during the broadcast time between midnight and 6 a.m., between 6 a.m. and 18 p.m., and also between 18 a.m. and 24 p.m. With this arrangement, we will achieve that radio stations will be able to meet the obligations of the law and at the same time implement their established programmes. Since we are Members of the Modern Centre Party and are also the proponents and signatories to the bill, we will, of course, support it. In this way, we have responded in a timely and effective manner to the legitimate warnings of the representatives of the public radio programmes.

In addition, it was explained by the proposers that the quota scope was not affected and that it had been established for many years, that a broad public debate had been opened on quotas and that a public presentation of opinions had been organised by the Committee on Culture, which included a number of coordinations. After the discussion on the individual articles of the bill, the Committee adopted the amendment of the SMC, Desus and SD to Article 1. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendment adopted, the text of the amended amendment is prepared on the basis of the first paragraph of Article 133 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u182	Honorable Presidents, colleagues and colleagues and representatives of the Government! The new Public Procurement Act, which entered into force on 1 April this year, significantly deteriorated the position of subcontractors compared to the previous Public Procurement Act. In 2010, the Law already laid down the compulsory direct payment of subcontractors by the contracting authority. Thus, the situation in the area of payment discipline in our country has improved, the beach of bankruptcy of smaller companies has been stopped and the activities of independent entrepreneurs have ceased. The new law on payments by subcontractors shall only revert to the contractual relationship between the main contractor and the subcontractor. In other words, the law allows the subcontractor to pay directly from the contracting authority, but only if the subcontractor is immediately agreed with the main contractor if he is alert. We know examples from practice. Sub-contractors will have to comply with the requirements of the main contractor as a stronger contracting party in order to be able to get to work at all and the direct payment of the subcontractor by the contracting authority is certainly not in the interest of the main contractor. The main contractor will therefore condition the subcontractor's participation in a public contract by not requiring direct payment. Otherwise, the subcontractor will not get the deal. In Novi Slovenije, the proposed amendment of the law again provides the legal basis for compulsory direct payment to subcontractors. The contracting authority, the main contractor and the subcontractor will have to provide documentation enabling direct payment. The main contractor must empower the contracting authority in the contract to pay the subcontractor directly to the subcontractor on the basis of a certified account or situation by the main contractor. The main operator must also submit to his account or situation an account or situation of the subcontractor, which he has previously confirmed. However, the subcontractor is obliged to submit an agreement on the basis of which the contracting entity settles its claim to the principal contractor instead of the main contractor. The proposal also limits the possibility of replacing the subcontractor after signing the contract with him for the purpose of lowering the price in such a way as to require his written consent to replace the subcontractor. If the main contractor is able to unilaterally replace its subcontractor at any time, it shall never have real security and shall ensure that it remains the contracting party of the main contractor until the end of the contract. However, the practice so far in procurement procedures in the field of construction, services and goods, including installation or installation works, has also shown that there are often cases where the supplier or main contractor does not have any capacity to carry out the contract and all works are awarded at lower prices to subcontractors. Since such indirect auctions are often without property, subcontractors cannot reach their payment for work done or for services. Therefore, in Novi Slovenija we propose that the main contractor must carry out at least 20% of the procurement with its own capacities. This will prevent the execution of a public contract to operators, so-called engineering companies we have known recently in Slovenia and have only been brokers in the procurement process. Since payment non-discipline or insolvency in our country poses a major problem, it is our concern and duty to protect weaker customers in procurement procedures. In this case, it is mainly small and micro-enterprises and independent entrepreneurs, as we say, so says the government, that they are the pillar of our economy. I therefore appeal to you, my fellow Members and colleagues, to support our proposal for amendments and the amendment of the law. These are entrepreneurs of small espionage who somehow want to compete in this market but do not get the chance. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u183	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary, Ministry of Public Administration mag. Tanya Rich.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u184	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Dear Members and Members! A good month and a half ago, the Public Procurement Act – ZJN 3 was prepared, taking into account a wide range of stakeholders. In preparing it, we have focused primarily on the purpose of this law, which is the economic and transparent use of public funds and the provision of competition. The main objective of the EU and our legislation is to allow all stakeholders free and equal access to contracting transactions. We have coordinated it with an extremely wide range of stakeholders, processed over four hundred pages of proposals and opinions and consulted with all interested economic associations and also aligned it with the general public. At the same time, we have also followed the view that the State must offer an opportunity to prevent abuses, but to enter into legal relations and infringements in proportion, where its measures must not lead to excessive bureaucracy or impose rules that are contrary to the European legal order. It should also be pointed out in the openings that JJN-3 also has an exceptional social note and, in particular, because of the prevention of social dumping and low prices, it has different tools, such as the obligation of operators to respect labour and social legislation and also provides for the termination of contracts in the event of a breach. The proceedings shall not involve entities which do not have a tax and social obligation to pay, have no forms of REK submitted or commit work-related offences and are legally convicted of a crime of infringement of workers' rights. In the drafting of the law, we have also taken into account, in the case of subcontractors, the opinion of the economy, the warnings of the profession and the problems of the judicial crack. The latter also point out that the regulation of direct payments by subcontractors is not necessary elsewhere, such as in a specific law such as the ZJN, since the Bond Code already regulates this and regulates the direct payment required in the Bond Code. However, many other measures are needed to prevent wage non-discipline. Addressing this issue mainly through public procurement has proved to be inadequate several times, and one of the positive measures, in our view, is the implementation of European law in the area of wage discipline. With regard to today's proposed amendments and the completion of the Public Procurement Act, the Government has adopted an opinion and does not support it in particular in view of the fact that the proposed Article 1 of the Act opposes the EU acquis, while at the same time acting on a proposal 2 of Article 2 of the Law, which does not, where necessary, interferes with the legal relationship, since it is the only one already regulated by the Bond Act. The government estimates that the current UNSCR is appropriate and does not deviate from the regulations of other EU Member States. In this context, the UNSC provides that direct payments by subcontractors are mandatory if required by the subcontractor. The law also requires the compulsory appointment of subcontractors in construction and services. In order to avoid potential abuse in business processes, in all contracts where the subcontractor does not require direct payment, the main contractor and the subcontractor will have to confirm receipt of payment by declaration. There is also a sanction for an offence where the perpetrator will be covered and also excluded from the procurement procedures for three or five years. However, if the subcontractor does not require direct payments under the ZJN-3, it may use all the institutes that are already provided by the Bond Code and also provide adequate support for payment, in contrast, which is particularly important in multi-annual contracts. Although the law is only valid for a good month and a half, it is noted that the contracting authorities already include warnings and instructions regarding subcontractors. This was also a key point of our ministry when we introduced the law in Slovenia, and we also agreed with interested associations that the implementation of the law should be monitored by all and that we should also be informed of possible complications to the contrary. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u186	Good afternoon, colleagues. I also welcome the guests from Stuttgart! The Public Procurement Act, as one of the most important systemic laws, must also provide adequate protection and protection to the parties in addition to the transparency and cost-effectiveness of the use of public funds.
