Unfortunately, our arguments were not heard at the time despite many comments. Christian Democrats are convinced that the actual payment to subcontractors should not be left to the will or contractual relationship between the contractor and the subcontractor. Sub-contractors should be provided with additional legal protection in such a way as to ensure their direct payment by law. This must be the responsibility of the contracting authority and the main contractor. It is true that the newly established Public Procurement Act provides for direct payment to subcontractors, but – pay attention! Only when the subcontractor explicitly requests this, but we all know the cases and we know that in practice subcontractors do not do this most often because at the time of the tendering, they do everything they can to win the deal. In practice, it is also often the case that the main contractors replace the subcontractor after the contract is concluded for the purpose of lowering prices. They can do this now without any subcontractor’s consent, which is unacceptable to us. The interest of each main contractor selected as the most favourable provider in the procurement process is always a profit. He will therefore do his utmost to obtain a subcontractor at the lowest possible price, even after he has already concluded a contract with a previously selected subcontractor. Of course, because all of this makes it possible for him to have an existing law. Another reason that may convince you, colleagues and colleagues to support this law is that at least 20% of the procurement must be carried out by the main contractor with its own capacities. The main contractors now appear in large cases companies that do not have their own assets or employees, but are only intermediaries in these transactions. The Public Procurement Act, which entered into force on 1 April this year, still allows for participation in public procurement procedures also for operators who do not have the capacity to carry out public procurement. When such an operator enters insolvency, subcontractors most often remain unpaid, and for many entrepreneurs, especially micro and small enterprises, it means dying on meals and ultimately their collapse. It also means the termination of the employment of a number of people who are employed by this subcontractor. As a result, the suppliers of this subcontractor also lose their payment. The question of responsibility in these cases remains so completely open and we are solving it in some way by means of this law. In view of all the above, in view of the bad examples of past practice, especially from the time of the construction of the motorway cross, when we have identified on specific occasions that subcontractors need to be further protected, we are proposing on such experiences to amend the new law on public procurement and we consider this novel to be necessary and more than justified, so of course we hope that you will support and adopt it.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u187	The word is given by the Group of the Modern Centre Party, for which Dr. Dragan Matić is a member.
ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u188	Mr President, respectable colleagues and colleagues! The current Public Procurement Act entered into force in early April of this year. However, the amendments come with a proposal to supplement the Public Procurement Act as early as the second month after its entry into force, before the new law came into practice. It is crucial that they wish to introduce a provision enabling the subcontractor to pay directly to subcontractors. This is proposed in such a way that the contracting authority, the main contractor and the subcontractor must provide the relevant documentation for the execution of the direct payment. However, the second major amendment they propose is that the main operators are prevented from only brokering or from doing business to all those who do not have their own capacity to participate in the execution of the contract. In this respect, the proposal submitted by New Slovenia also provides that the main contractor must carry out at least 20% of the public procurement. At the time of consideration of the current Public Procurement Act, the most time in the debate was devoted to the content of how to properly regulate the obligation of the main contractor to pay the services provided and to sell the goods by subcontractors. Content-like provisions, as now contained in the current law, have been proposed by a committee hearing by the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia. Minister of Public Administration, Mr Koprivnikar said at the meeting of the Committee that they had a broad public debate during the drafting of the law, had countless meetings and coordinations with a number of interested associations and all of these meetings, and all of these meetings referred in the majority to the relationship between the main operators and subcontractors and to the regulation of their mutual relations. As a consequence, the applicable Public Procurement Act in Article 94 contains a compromise solution allowing subcontractors to request direct payment by the contracting authority or to decide that the principal contractor is responsible for their payment. Furthermore, the applicable Public Procurement Act already provides that the contracting authority may require the tenderer to perform the key tasks on his own. Restricting works in a precise percentage, as provided for in the proposal for a law submitted by NSI, would be contrary to the procurement directive. As stated, direct payments under the new Public Procurement Act are always mandatory as required by the subcontractor. In practice, this is done by empowering the main contractor in the contract to pay the subcontractor directly to the subcontractor on the basis of a certified account. However, the subcontractor must submit an agreement on the basis of which the contracting authority shall, instead of the tenderer, settle the payment to the subcontractor. The applicable Public Procurement Act already regulates direct payments to subcontractors and therefore this amendment, as proposed by Nova Slovenije, is unnecessary. In the case of public contracts where the subcontractor does not require direct payment, the main contractor and the subcontractor must confirm receipt of the payment by declaration. In cases where the subcontractor is not paid, the Public Procurement Act lays down a fine and a sanction for such operators. The sanctions are very severe, namely, the non-payments are disabled in running for public contracts for – pay attention! three years in the case of the procurement of goods and services or five years in the procurement of works. Therefore, the deterrent measure for non-payment is indeed very sharp and there is no doubt that it will be effective. In addition to the above, it should be pointed out that the legal theory already states that the mandatory implementation of direct payments to subcontractors in the Public Procurement Act is unnecessary, since this is already governed by the Bond Code in Article 631 under which direct payment is compulsory. This Article provides for the possibility for the subcontractor to request direct payment from the contracting authority from the amount still owed by the contracting authority to the principal operator at the actual time of the request. So it is also an appropriate instrument for all projects that last for years or are long-term. It should be noted that, in the past, parallel or double legal arrangements have led to confusion and individual abuses both in the Public Procurement Act and in the Bond Code. The current law on public procurement has put an end to this confusion. In view of all the above, the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre cannot support the proposal of the law, since we consider it unnecessary, does not bring any additional solutions in the direction that the applicants declare or constitute a step backwards. Thank you.

This is problematic from the point of view of responsibility. Such restrictions should also apply to cases of all companies in the case of a group of economic operators who have applied as a group of entities. Of course, clean intermediary companies are supposed to be excluded from this circle. The amendment of this Act also seeks to introduce a mandatory direct payment to subcontractors by the contracting authority and, in this regard, the obligations of the contracting authority of the main contractor and the subcontractor to submit the relevant documentation enabling such direct payment. The main contractor should already have, at the time of signature, a contract for the execution of a contract with the contracting authority to conclude all contracts with subcontractors and should submit them to the contracting authority within five days. However, should there be a replacement of the subcontractor during the execution of the contract, the main contractor should submit the contract to the contracting authority within five days. In order to prevent the possibility of sub-contracting, the possibility of replacing the subcontractor in order to reduce the contractual value of supplies of goods or services of the subcontractor without its written consent would thus be limited. In our view, it is essential to prevent the continuation of bad practices when the subcontractor is already preparing for the execution of works or supplies of goods, but the main contractor requires continued cooperation in the implementation of the contract with an additional price reduction. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, when adopting the existing law last November, we agreed with all the comments and proposals submitted by the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia and the Chamber of Commerce and Industry of Slovenia, which pointed out all these shortcomings and traps. However, no comment was taken into account by the interested profession or opposition. Otherwise, it is nothing new, for the authorities of the profession and the opposition do not take into account so much in any field. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the draft law on amendments and additions to the Public Procurement Act.

ParlaMint-SI_2016-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-19.ana.u255	Thank you very much for the words, honourable Members. On the basis of the confirmed views of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia and on the basis of the authority of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, the State Secretary at the Ministry of Infrastructure and Space, Mr Ljubo Žnidar, signed an agreement in Baku between the Government of the Republic of Slovenia and the Government of the Republic of Azerbaijan on international road transport. The reason for the conclusion of the agreement is the establishment of conditions and a way for the continued smooth carriage of passengers and goods between the country and in transit across their territories. The agreement is based on the principle of reciprocity and the need to develop road transport between countries. The carriage of goods between the territories of the two countries shall be carried out on the basis of licences. However, the transport of passengers is subject to an authorisation system. The Agreement also regulates occasional and interchangeable transport, which is also subject to the authorisation system. For the proper implementation of the provisions of the Agreement and the resolution of open issues, a Joint Committee shall be established, consisting of representatives of the competent authorities of both Slovenia and Azerbaijan. The Joint Committee shall meet at the request of one of the States Parties to the Agreement and the meetings shall alternately be in the territory of one State and another. Both parties agreed to register the agreement with the United Nations General Secretariat. Thank you.
